### 16.3 The coverage rule from the raw files (`c43` §43.3)

The rule as built, copied from `pipeline/local_districts.py` and D3's §32 into my own summary of each raw file (`cover_summary`, cached as `fifth/derived/<file>.cover.json`): the county's top contest is the candidate contest with votes in the most precincts; a county is short where the US House's rows (by district) miss more than 2% of the top contest's precincts holding more than 2% of its votes (the missed set capped by the count difference, names matched by the build's `_loose` rule); a state that left a US House seat off the ballot is not read; a contest whose own rows are in the county's precincts within 2% is released; a statewide contest (rows in 90% of the state's counties) is held where the votes its rows miss are over 2% of the state's, else noted. Against `check-districts.json` [measured]:
- **Counties short by the US House's rows:** 2018, 6 and 6 the same (Maine 23011; New York 36027, 36043, 36057, 36083, 36119), each with the same top contest, precincts, missed count and share, chambers' counts and released contests; 2024, 3 and 3 the same (Indiana 18017, 18161; New York 36055); 2022, D3's 3 (Michigan 26015, 26111; New York 36111, the same) and mine 4: **Wayne County (26163) by my count alone**, the US House's rows missing 22 of 1,089 precincts (2.02%) holding 2.12% of the Secretary of State's votes, a hair over both lines where D3's count is under; the cause is spelling, the US House and House rows writing 'Charter Township of Brownston' where the top contest writes 'Brownstown', so the name match misses 14 precincts either way.
- **Statewide contests short in a county:** 2018, 18 and 18, the same share and verdict (held: Connecticut's registrars, Hawaii's US House 2); 2024, 50 and 50, 49 the same (held the same six: Connecticut's registrars, Hawaii's US House 2, New Hampshire's commissioner 3 and US House 2, New York's Proposal One at 3.81%, Rhode Island's US House; Ohio's county recorder noted on both sides, D3 adding Miami County's 87 precincts); 2022, 34 and 34, 32 shared, 31 the same: D3 only, Idaho's governor and Secretary of State in Power County (noted, 0.28%); mine only, Vermont's 'STATE HOUSE|' and 'STATE SENATE|' (rows with an empty district code, which my key reads as a statewide contest and D3's family rule as the chamber's: my keying, not a difference in the records); and **Michigan's Attorney General**, mine held at 5.2% of the state with Wayne County, D3's noted at 0.05% with Arenac alone (below).
- **The gate's records at d680bd4:** New York 2024 Assembly 130, 133, 134, 135, 136, 137, 138, 139 and Proposal One: p before (1.96e-5 … 0) and None after, the reason in `p.not_computed`, no `held_reason` key (as every held legislative record at this commit).
- **Wayne County 2022, read whole** (MEDSL's `2022-mi-local-precinct-general.zip`) [measured]: the Attorney General's rows miss 187 of the Secretary of State's 1,094 precinct names (Brownstown and others), 36% of the SoS's votes there, and the rows present sum to 3,023,719 against the SoS's 1,285,281 over the same modes (2.35×); no p rests on them (the AG record's count is MEDSL's statewide 6,146,375, p none). Wayne's Senate lines for districts 3 and 10 carry no candidate name, party only (SD3 41,343 votes, SD10 24,805), so the named sums lack Wayne: with those lines SD3 is 71,650 against Klarner's 71,490 (+0.2%) and SD10 89,185 against 89,185 (equal), both held by #100 at −57.6% and −27.8%. House 10: 12,541 votes sit on the WRITE-IN line where Klarner gives Corcoran 12,948 (named 391); all lines 41,033 against Klarner's 41,012 (+0.05%); held at −30.5%. Senate 35: Midland (26111) is a gate-short county whose Senate rows carry only District 34's candidates, so the district's counties by MEDSL's own rows lack Midland and the gate does not reach it; held at −25.9%, the gap about Midland's part. House 27 (−14.5%; MEDSL's rows in Gibraltar, Riverview, Southgate, Trenton, Wyandotte and Grosse Ile, Brownstown's House rows coded 028), Senate 12 (−7.0%) and House 99 (−11.7%) I cannot explain from the file. ## 17. Virginia 2023's gate, read whole from the Department's files (`c45_va_gate.py`)

The orchestrator (01:12 UTC on the 13th, on merging #104): "Put Virginia's gate whole in section 17. Merge main into your branch, give it its own run, and open a new PR. In that section, read the option keys of your four write-in-over-ballot-name records (Charlotte's Cullen/Red House supervisor, Clifton's mayor and council, Dillwyn's council). They are the test I set D3 under (d): a name-keyed option that beats a full ballot means the write-in inference fails there. Say for each whether it holds. The two sheriffs listed against unopposed treasurers are as I ruled. D3's next PR will build that, so check it when it lands."

### 17.5 The option keys; the four records (`c45` §45.5)

The file's local contests hold 2,269 `cs` options, 1,440 `wi-` lines, 78 name-keyed options in 65 contests (D3: 65) and 18 question options; the name-keyed ids are the candidate's name run together with a number (`MargaretElainePearson-1-`, `LiamJ.Watson-3-`) [measured]. The four records of §16.8, where a named write-in outpolls a ballot name, under the orchestrator's test (a name-keyed option that beats a full ballot means the write-in inference fails there):
- **Charlotte County, Member Board of Supervisors (Cullen/Red House District)**, 1 seat, 1 precinct: `cs395` M. Anthony "Tony" Reeves 227 (the one ballot name, a full ballot); `HenryW.Carwile,Jr.-3-` Henry W. Carwile, Jr. 304 and `HenryW.Carwile,Jr.-2-` named "Write In" 4, both keyed by Carwile's name, and no `wi-` line in the contest. Carwile's 304 beats the full ballot: **the inference fails the test here.** (What the file shows beside it: the contest's write-in line itself is keyed by Carwile's name and labelled "Write In", so the file keys the whole write-in count of this contest by the one name adjudicated; the record reads Reeves as the one name on the ballot and the contest not computed for strong write-ins, 134% of the last winner.)
- **Fairfax County, Mayor (Town of Clifton)**, 1 seat, 1 precinct: `cs67` William R. Hollaway 62 (a full ballot); `TomPeterson-2-` Thomas C. Peterson 67; `wi-cc43` Write-In 2. Peterson's 67 beats the full ballot: **fails the test here.**
- **Fairfax County, Member Town Council (Town of Clifton)**, 5 seats, 1 precinct: five `cs` names (McDonald 98, Screen 97, Davis 77, Effros 65, Poe 48; a full ballot); `MaryM.Hess-…` Mary M. Hess 71; `wi-` Write-In 15. Hess's 71 beats Effros and Poe: **fails the test here.**
- **Buckingham County, Member Town Council (Dillwyn)**, 6 seats, 1 precinct: three `cs` names (Reams 61, Baker 60, Venable 53) for six seats, not a full ballot; `SandraF.Moss-3-` 75 and `FayeW.Shumaker-2-` 68 name-keyed; and the contest's `wi-cc1541` write-in line itself named "Robert L. Pinelli" 63 (a person's name on the `wi-` line, read by its key as the line). Nothing beats a full ballot: **holds.**
Each name-keyed option's votes lie in the one precinct of its town or district, not spread; the file gives no party for any of them. What the test cannot say is whether Carwile, Peterson and Hess were on the ballot or were write-ins who won: the file's own keying ("Write In" under Carwile's name; a `wi-` line beside Peterson and Hess) reads as adjudicated write-ins, and the page does not show these one-locality contests. That is for D3 under the orchestrator's ruling; the records as built read them as write-ins, so Reeves's, Hollaway's and the Clifton five's contests are not computed for strong write-ins rather than contested, and no p rests on the reading.

### 17.7 Readings

- The gate holds whole as D3 recorded it: (a) 181 of 183 and the two held, (b) 42 of 43 and the one held, (c) 1,405 and 43, (d) 1,448 ratios and verdicts equal. The records' every name, vote, seat and write-in flag is the file's.
- The write-in inference fails the orchestrator's test in three contests (Charlotte's Cullen/Red House supervisor, Clifton's mayor and council) and holds in Dillwyn; the file's keys in those three are a write-in's (a "Write In" option under Carwile's name; `wi-` lines beside Peterson and Hess), so what fails is the inference that a name-keyed option cannot be a ballot name, not any count. The three records are not computed either way (strong write-ins); a ruling that reads them as contested would give them the no-party class, also not computed.
- Dillwyn's `wi-` line carries a person's name ("Robert L. Pinelli"), the only such line among the 1,440; the record lists him as the write-in line by its key.

### 19.3 The refit (`c47` §47.3)

My own fit (`m2fit`, 5,047 rows after D3's counts are applied: the four new counts and their later lags, West Virginia 2012 dropped, West Virginia 2016's lag at 0.512972) reproduces the blocks before and after in every even year 2010–2024: the largest relative difference of any term from the file's block is 1.5 × 10⁻⁴ (c₀, 2022); all 64 term changes before → after have the same sign as the blocks'. Of the 126 governor p that moved: 109 of the model's own in the even years are within 10⁻⁴ of my ratio (the new forecast's f(½) and the record's n) and move the same way; 3 with 538's p of record move by n alone (Connecticut 2018, New York 2018, Indiana 2024: ×0.967836, ×0.915789, ×0.988859); 13 odd-year records and Utah 2008 recompute from the record's own forecast before and after (density: f(½)/(2n)), e.g. Utah 2008 ×0.951457 from n 875,865 → 920,551 at the same forecast. The odd years' blocks move with the even fits (2021's national term 3.15 × 10⁻⁴ → 3.74 × 10⁻⁵, the rest by under 1%). `data/house/2012.json`'s block changes in its near-tie test alone (5 leaves) [measured].

### 20.2 The unopposed comparator (`c49` §49.2)

D3's rule, copied: a comparator's figure is unopposed where its contest has no more names on the ballot than seats, write-in lines aside; a chamber's lines summed over a locality are unopposed if any of its districts there is; the line is 1.5 against an unopposed figure, 1.1 otherwise; held above the line, listed above 1.0 up to it.

- **Virginia 2023** [measured, from the 2023 export, sha equal to D3's]: 1,448 contests beside the legislature's; 818 top figures per locality, 464 of them unopposed.
